Well-posedness of Generalized Fractional Singular Burgers equation driven by | D | 1 2 ​ ξ |D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi
ShuoLin Zhang, Zhaonan Luo, Zhaoyang Yin

Abstract
In this paper, we study the generalized solution of Fractional Singular Burgers equation driving by | D | 1 2 ​ ξ |D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi . We establish a framework to describe the equations satisfied by generalized solutions, termed the Generalized Fractional Singular Burgers equation(GFSB), and prove its local well-posedness. Finally, we prove that the solution of GFSB can be the generalized solution of Fractional Singular Burgers equation for γ > 3 2 \gamma>\frac{3}{2} .



1
Introduction

In this paper, we will consider Fractional Singular Burgers (FSB) equation driven by singular noise
|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi
with weak dissipation
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
in
W^{s}(\mathbb{T})=\mathcal{C}^{s}(\mathbb{T})\cap W^{s}(\mathbb{T})
space

\partial_{t}u-\Lambda^{\gamma}u=\partial_{x}(u^{2})+|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi

(1.1)

where
\xi
is a centered Gaussian space-time random distribution with covariance,
\mathbb{T}
is some toru satisfied
\int_{\mathbb{T}}e^{ikx}=0
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}
.

\mathbb{E}[\xi(\omega,t,x)\xi(\omega,s,y)]=\delta(t-s)\delta(x-y),\quad t,s\geq 0,\quad x,y\in\mathbb{R}

(1.2)

Consider a more general case

\partial_{t}u-\Lambda^{\gamma}u=\partial_{x}(u^{2})+|D|^{\beta}\xi

(1.3)

In our previous work
[
ZLY25
]
, we study the case of
\beta=0
and
\gamma\in(\frac{4}{3},2]
(can be improved to
\gamma\in(1,2]
but in the
C^{s}\cap H^{s}
space) and give a conjecture of relation between
\gamma
and
\beta
as follows

\gamma\geq\beta+1

(1.4)

The well-posedness in the case of
\gamma=2,\beta=1
for
\mathcal{C}^{s}
space can be refer to
[
GP17
]
and the uniqueness of energy solution can be refer to
[
GP18
]
. In this paper, we extend the results to the fractional case
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
and, following our earlier approach
[
ZLY25
]
, establish well-posedness in the
W^{s}=H^{s}\cap\mathcal{C}^{s}
spaces.

1.1
Generalized solution

Compare to our previous work, for
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
,
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
, equation (
1.3
) is ill defined, since
\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}(u^{2})
has no meaning when
u\in\mathcal{C}^{0-}
or
H^{0-}
. To overcome this problem, a common approach is to study its generalized solution. More preciesly, let
u^{\epsilon}
satisfied the approximated equation

\partial_{t}u^{\epsilon}-\Lambda^{\gamma}u^{\epsilon}=\partial_{x}(u^{\epsilon})^{2}+|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{\epsilon},\quad u^{\epsilon}(0)=u^{\epsilon}_{0}+Y_{\epsilon}(0).

(1.5)

If we can prove that
u^{\epsilon}
has a limit
u
in some regularity space, then we call
u
be the generalized solution of (
1.3
)(See
[
GIP15
,
GP17
,
Hai13
]
). To be more specific, let
Y_{\epsilon}
be the solution of linear equation

\partial_{t}Y_{\epsilon}-\Lambda^{\gamma}Y_{\epsilon}=|D|^{\beta}\xi_{\epsilon},

(1.6)

with
Y_{\epsilon}(0)=\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-s\Lambda^{\gamma}}|D|^{\beta}\xi_{\epsilon}(s)\mathrm{d}s
. Then
u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}=u_{\epsilon}-Y_{\epsilon}
satisfied the difference equation

\partial_{t}u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}-\Lambda^{\gamma}u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}=D((u^{(1)}_{\epsilon})^{2}+2u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}Y_{\epsilon}+Y_{\epsilon}^{2}).

(1.7)

Note equation (
1.7
) is always well-defined and we can obtain a smooth solution
u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}
in
W^{s}=H^{s}\cap\mathcal{C}^{s}
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
which determined by
u_{0}^{\epsilon}
and
Y_{\epsilon}
. It can back to the equation (
1.3
) in the regular case, for example
\beta=0
and
\gamma>\frac{5}{4}
in our previous work
[
ZLY25
]
, since
Y_{\epsilon}
can converge to
Y
in the
W^{\alpha}
for
\alpha
is nonnegative. But its nontrivial in irregular case such as
Y\in C_{T}W^{0-}
since
Y\cdot Y
is ill-defined. One useful method is to define
u^{(1)}=u^{(2)}+Y^{\hbox to7.99pt{\vbox to4.19pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.99448pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

is the limit of
\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Lambda^{\gamma}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}(Y_{\epsilon}^{2})\mathrm{d}s
when
\epsilon\to 0
. We need to define a new random distribution allow our analysis to proceed and iterate such argument for
u_{\epsilon}^{(2)},u_{\epsilon}^{(3)}
(See Section
4
) until the high order term can be satisfied the well-defined equation. Such method is firstly used in
[
Hai13
]
to solve KPZ equation. Its a type of heat equation with nonlinearity
(\partial_{x}u)^{2}
as follows

\partial_{t}u-\Delta u=(\partial_{x}u)^{2}+\xi.

(1.8)

The authors establish a sequence of random distribution such as
\{Y^{\tau}\}_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}
and prove there exists a generalized solution
u
which can be written as
u=\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}c(\tau)Y^{\tau}+v
where
v
is the high order term. Our main result is following

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
, given a approximation equation

\partial_{t}u^{\epsilon}-\Lambda^{\gamma}u^{\epsilon}=\partial_{x}(u^{\epsilon})^{2}+|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{\epsilon},\quad u^{\epsilon}(0)=u^{\epsilon}_{0}+Y_{\epsilon}(0).

(1.9)

there exists
u\in C_{T}W^{-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}
such that
u_{\epsilon}\to u
in
C_{T}W^{-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}
in probability sense for some small
\delta>0
. Moreover,
u
has a structure

To prove this conclusion, our strategy is to formulate an abstract equation(See (
1.11
) and (
1.14
)), establish its well-posedness, and then demonstrate that, for appropriately chosen parameters(named enhanced data
\mathbb{X}
), the solution of the abstract equation coincides with the limit of the approximating solution (
1.9
). Compared to a direct approach, this method offers two advantages: (1) it provides an equation satisfied by the generalized solution, and (2) it allows us to study the generalized solution directly using PDE theory, thereby reducing the complexity inherent in asymptotic analysis.

1.2
Generalized Fractional Singular Burgers equation

In this section, we will construct a we will build such random distribution when
\gamma>\frac{3}{2}
be fractional and
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
.
Given a definition of Generalize Rough Burgers(GRB) equation driven by enhance data
\mathbb{X}
, and prove its local well-posedness. Similar work can also be found in
[
Hai14
]
, where the authors construct a generalized equation in the Modelled distribution space
\mathcal{D}_{p}^{\alpha}
and use the reconstruction operator backing to classical space
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
[See Section 7 in
[
Hai14
]
]. We will show that its solution is the limit of (
1.9
) when enhance data
\mathbb{X}
is determined by some Gaussian process. We first give the regular process of regular sequence and enhance data.

Definition 1.1
.

Denote a set
\mathcal{T}(\tau^{*}):=\{\tau_{1}\cdot\tau_{2}:\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathcal{T}\}\cup\{1,\tau^{*}\}
which is equipped with a defined commutative multiplication operation
\cdot
. We say
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(\tau^{*})
is a regular subset of
\mathcal{T}(\tau^{*})
if
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(\tau^{*})
satisfied following two properties

•

(a)
\tau^{*}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
,
1\cdot\tau=\tau\cdot 1=\tau
.

•

(b) There exists an order on
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
such that for every
\tau_{k}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
can be represent by
\tau_{1}\cdot\tau_{2}
where
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\{\tau_{i}\}_{i<k}
.

Define a function
r:\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, we say its a
b
-order(
b>0
) regular function on
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, if

r(\tau_{1}\cdot\tau_{2})=\min(r(\tau_{1}),r(\tau_{2}),r(\tau_{1})+r(\tau_{2}))+b

For our convenient, we pack above in a triple

Definition 1.2
.

Define a triple
(V,\mathcal{T},b)
, where
\mathcal{T}
satisfied Definition
1.1
and
b\geq 0
, let
r
be a
b
-order regular function on
\mathcal{T}
. Let
V:\eta\mapsto V_{\eta}
where
\{V_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in\mathbb{R}}
is a sequence of Banach space satisfied for
\eta\leq\bar{\eta}
,
V_{\bar{\eta}}\subset V_{\eta}
. Then we say that
X
is adapted to the triple
(V,\mathcal{T},b)
, if following two properties is satisfied

(1)

X:\mathcal{T}\mapsto\cup_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}
.

(2)

X^{\tau}:=X(\tau)\in V_{r(\tau)}
.

Finally, we define a enhance data
\mathbb{X}
is generated by
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}
if following two properties is satisfied

(1)

\mathbb{X}
is a subset of
X|_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}
, where
X
is adapted to triple
(V,\mathcal{T},b)
.

(2)

X^{\tau^{*}}\in\mathbb{X}
.

(3)

For any
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
satisfied
r(\tau_{1})+r(\tau_{2})<0
, we have
X^{\tau_{1}\cdot\tau_{2}}\in\mathbb{X}
.
Since every thing in
\mathbb{X}
is belong to some Banach space, we define the
X^{\alpha,b}
norm for
\mathbb{X}
as

\|\mathbb{X}\|_{X^{\alpha,b}}=\max_{X^{\tau}\in\mathbb{X}}\{\|X^{\tau}\|_{V_{r(\tau)}}:r(\tau^{*})=\alpha\}

(1.10)

where
r
be a
b
-order regular function.

We say a regular set
\mathcal{R}:=\{(\tau_{1},\tau_{2}):\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(\tau^{*})\}
associated with
\mathcal{T}
is the maximal set satisfied for any
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\in\mathcal{R}
, we have
r(\tau_{1})+r(\tau_{2})>0
, with help of above definitions and Definition
1.1
, we can give our definition of GFSB equation.

Definition 1.3
.

Given an enhance data
\mathbb{X}\in X_{T}^{\alpha,b}
generated by
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(\tau^{*})
, we say
u
is the solution of
subcritical GFSB
equation driven by
(\mathbb{X},u_{0})
, if
u
can be written as
u:=\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}c(\tau)X^{\tau}+v
, where
v
is the solution of

\partial_{t}v-\Lambda^{\gamma}v=D(v^{2})+D(\sum_{(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}}c(\tau)c(\tau^{\prime})X^{\tau}X^{\tau^{\prime}})^{2}+2D(v\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}c(\tau)X^{\tau})

(1.11)

We call it subcritical, its well-defined only for
2\alpha+b>0
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
\alpha<0
,
\gamma>1
and
0<b\leq\gamma-1
satisfied

2\alpha+b>0.

Given
\mathbb{X}
be a
b
-order enhance data generated by a regular set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
satisfied
(\mathbb{X},u_{0})\in X_{T}^{\alpha}\times W^{2s}
, then we have

•

(Existence and uniqueness)then there exists
0<s<\rho=\alpha+b
, such that there exist
T^{*}
such that exists a unique
v\in\mathcal{W}^{s}(T^{*})
satisfied (
1.11
) for
v(0)=u_{0}
.

•

(Blow up condition)The maximal time of existence
\bar{T}
of such a solution does not dependent on
s
and satisfied

\bar{T}=\sup\{t>0;\|v\|_{\mathcal{W}^{s}(t)}<\infty\}

(1.12)

•

(Continuous dependence on initial data)Let
v^{1}
and
v^{2}
be two solutions driven by
(\mathbb{X}^{1},u_{0}^{1})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{2},u_{0}^{2})
, for any
0<t\leq T<\bar{T}
we have following estimate

\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{s}(t)}\leq C(\|u_{1}(0)-u_{2}(0)\|_{W^{s}}+\|\mathbb{X}^{1}-\mathbb{X}^{2}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}{T}^{\frac{\delta}{\gamma}})

(1.13)

where
C,M
is dependent on
\|\mathbb{X}^{1}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}
,
\|\mathbb{X}^{2}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}
,
\|v^{1}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{s}(T)}
,
\|v^{2}\|_{C_{T}W^{s}}
and
T
.

Remark 1.1
.

Note that equation (
1.14
) is well-defined if and only if
v\cdot X^{\tau^{*}}
is well defined. Sometimes this does not hold, for example
\gamma=2
and
\alpha=-\frac{1}{2}-\delta
(See more details in
[
GP17
]
). In such cases, we must rely on para-controlled ideas to define the generalized equation which will be shown in the next theorem. Such method also can be used in subcritical case for improving the regularity of remainder term.

•

There exists
\tau_{\downarrow}\in\mathcal{T}
such that
\tau_{m}=\tau^{*}\cdot\tau_{\downarrow}
.

•

r(\tau_{m}):=r_{m}=r(\tau^{*})+b
, where
r
is a
b
-order regular function.

When
2\alpha+b<0
, the third term of the right hand side of (
1.11
) and
X^{\tau_{m}}\cdot X^{\tau^{*}}
is ill-defined, ‘it mainly comes from
v\cdot X^{\tau^{*}}
. We need to use a more refined analysis to divide the difficult term(See
[
ZLY25
,
GIP15
,
GP17
]
.) which help us to realize that
v\prec X^{\tau^{*}}
is well-defined but lower regularity, and
v\circ X^{\tau^{*}}
is ill-defined. Inspirited by
[
GIP15
,
GP17
]
, we need to redefined such
v\circ X^{\tau^{*}}
by ansatz that
v=u^{\prime}\prec\mathcal{Q}
and
v\circ X^{\tau^{*}}
can be reformulated by
[u^{\prime}\prec\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}-u^{\prime}\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}]+u^{\prime}\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}
where the first term gains high regularity (See
[
GP17
]
or Lemma B.2 in
[
ZLY25
]
). And the second term is a promote. Such controlled rough path theory firstly raised in
[
Gub04
]
. For adapting to such structure, we construct a para-controlled type GSB equation. Denote a
\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
satisfied following two properties

Its obviously that
\tau_{m}
always exists.

Definition 1.4
.

Given enhance data
\mathbb{X}(t)\in X_{T}^{\alpha,b}
generalized by a regular set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, where
\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. We say
u:=\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}c(\tau)X^{\tau}+u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q}+u^{\sharp}
be the solution of GFSB equation driven by
(\mathbb{X},u_{0})
if
(u,u^{\prime},u^{\sharp})
on
[0,T]
satisfied

\displaystyle u^{\prime}=

\displaystyle c(\tau_{m})X^{\tau_{m}}+u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q}+u^{\sharp}

(1.14)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\sharp}-\Lambda^{\gamma}u^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle D(u^{\mathcal{Q}})^{2}+D(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\cdot\tau^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\\
\tau,\tau^{\prime}\neq\tau^{*}\end{subarray}}c(\tau)c(\tau^{\prime})X^{\tau}X^{\tau^{\prime}})^{2}+2D(u^{\mathcal{Q}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}/{\tau^{*}}}c(\tau)X^{\tau})+\mathcal{L}(\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}c(\tau)X^{\tau})

\displaystyle+2[D,u^{\prime}\prec]X^{\tau^{*}}+\mathcal{I}(X^{\tau_{m}},X^{\tau^{*}})+\mathcal{J}(u^{\mathcal{Q}},X^{\tau^{*}})+\mathcal{K}(u^{\prime},\mathcal{Q})+\mathcal{M}(u^{\sharp},X^{\tau^{*}})+u^{\prime}\widetilde{X}.

where
u(0):=u_{0}+\sum_{\tau\in M}c(\tau)X^{\tau}(0)
,
u^{\mathcal{Q}}
is define as
u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q}+u^{\sharp}
and
\mathcal{Q}:=\int_{0}^{t}P(t-s)D(X^{\tau^{*}}(s))\mathrm{d}s
.
\mathcal{L}=\partial_{t}-\Lambda^{\gamma}
,
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J},\mathcal{K},\mathcal{M}
are linear operators,
\widetilde{X}
is random distribution.

and obtain the local result,

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
\alpha<0
,
\gamma>1
and
0<b\leq\gamma-1
satisfied ,

\alpha+b>0.

Given an enhance data
\mathbb{X}(t)\in X_{T}^{\alpha,b}
generalized by a regular set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, where
\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and
u_{0}\in X_{T}^{\alpha}\times W^{2s}
. If for
\rho=\alpha+b
, we have following properties

•

One has
\mathcal{I}
is a bounded linear operator from
\mathcal{W}^{\rho}(T)\times\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}(T)
to
C_{T}W^{s+\alpha-1}
.

•

One has

is a bounded linear operator from
\mathcal{W}^{s}(T)\times\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}(T)

•

One has
\mathcal{K}
is a

bounded linear operator from
\mathcal{W}^{s}(T)\times\mathcal{W}^{s}(T)\times\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}(T)
to
C_{T}W^{s+\alpha-1}
.

•

One has
\mathcal{M}
is a bounded linear operator from
\mathcal{W}^{2s}(T)\times\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}(T)
to
C_{T}W^{s+\alpha-1}
.

and
\|\tilde{X}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2\alpha+b}(T)}\lesssim\|\mathbb{X}\|^{2}_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}
. Then for any
0<s<\rho
, we have

•

(Existence and uniqueness)there exist
T^{*}
such that a unique triple
(u,u^{\prime},u^{\sharp})\in\mathcal{W}_{\infty}^{\alpha}{(T^{*})}\times\mathcal{W}^{s}{(T^{*})}\times\mathcal{W}^{2s}{(T^{*})}
satisfied equation (
1.14
).

•

(Blow up condition)The maximal time of existence
\bar{T}
of such a solution does not dependent on

2
Basic tool

We first introduce some notations. Define Hölder space as
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in(0,1)
as
and
H^{\alpha}
space as ,
W^{\alpha}=\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}\cap H^{\alpha}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}(T)=
and for any
T\geq 0
, we introduce a class of temporal regularity spaces, which have been referenced in
[
GIP15
,
GP17
,
Hai13
,
CC18
]
. Define the such norm with time
T
as follows.
C_{T}X=C([0,T],X)
for the space of continuous maps from
[0,T]
to
X
, equipped with the norm
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\cdot\|_{X}
. For
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we also define
C_{T}^{\alpha}X
as a space of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous continuous functions from
[0,T]
to
X
equipped with the norm
\|f\|_{C_{T}^{\alpha}X}=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\|f_{s,t}\|_{X}}{|t-s|^{\alpha}}
.

\mathcal{W}^{0}(T)=C_{T}(L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}),\quad\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}(T)=C_{T}W^{\alpha}\cap C^{\frac{\alpha}{\gamma}}_{T}W^{0}.

For such space, we have following Schauder estimate

Lemma 2.1

(
[
ZLY25
]
)
.

Let
1\leq p,r\leq\infty
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
\gamma\geq 1
. If
f(0)\in W^{\alpha+\delta}
,
\mathcal{L}f:=(\partial_{t}-\Lambda^{\gamma})f\in W^{\alpha}
, then we have following estimate

\|f\|_{W^{\alpha+\delta}}\leq C\|f(0)\|_{W^{\alpha+\delta}}+Ct^{1-\frac{\delta}{\gamma}}\|\mathcal{L}f\|_{W^{\alpha}}

(2.1)

Moreover, if
\mathcal{L}f\in C_{T}W^{\alpha}
, for
\alpha\in(0,\gamma)
and
\delta\in[0,\gamma)
satisfied
0<\alpha+\delta<\gamma
and
T\leq 1
, we have the estimate

\|f\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\alpha+\delta}(T)}\leq C\|f(0)\|_{W^{\alpha+\delta}}+CT^{1-\frac{\delta}{\gamma}}\|\mathcal{L}f\|_{C_{T}W^{\alpha}}.

(2.2)

2.1
Bony para-product

Let
f
and
g
belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
, by Littlewood-Paley decomposition, it can be present as

f=\sum_{j\geq-1}\Delta_{j}g\quad and\quad g=\sum_{j\geq-1}\Delta_{j}g

where
\Delta_{j}
is a Fourier operator which is support on
2^{j}\mathcal{C}
for some fixed annual
\mathcal{C}
and any
j\geq 0
, and support on a Ball for
j=-1
, see more details in
[
BCD11
,
ZLY25
]
. Then we define the
nonhomogeneous paraproduct
between
f
and
g
as

f\prec g=\sum_{j}S_{j-1}f\Delta_{j}g

(2.3)

and the nonhomogeneous remainder of
f
and
g
as

f\circ g=\sum_{|i-j|\leq 1}\Delta_{i}f\Delta_{j}g.

(2.4)

Let
f\succ g=g\prec f
, then its easy to check that
fg
has following decomposition

fg=f\prec g+f\succ g+f\circ g.

(2.5)

We also introduce a modified version of the para-product, which is useful for para-controlled method. Let
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}_{+})
be nonnegative with compact support contained in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and with total mass 1, define
Q_{i}
for all
i\geq 1
as follows

\displaystyle Q_{i}:C\mathcal{C}^{\beta}\to C\mathcal{C}^{\beta},\quad Q_{i}f(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}2^{-\gamma i}\varphi(2^{\gamma i}(t-s))f(s\vee 0)\mathrm{d}s.

with the help of
Q_{i}
, we define a modified para-product

f\prec\!\!\!\prec g=\sum_{i}(Q_{i}S_{i-1}f)\Delta_{i}g.

(2.6)

Lemma 2.2

(
[
BCD11
]
)
.

Define
f\prec g:=\sum_{j}S_{j-1}f\Delta_{j}g
,
f\succ g=g\prec f
,
f\circ g:=\sum_{|k-j|\leq 1}\Delta_{k}f\Delta_{j}g
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have following estimate

•

1.

\|f\prec g\|_{W^{s}}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|D^{k}g\|_{W^{s-k}},\quad\|f\prec\!\!\!\prec g(t)\|_{W^{\beta}}\leq C\|f\|_{C_{t}L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{W^{\beta}}.

•

2. If
t<0
and
\frac{1}{r}=\min(1,\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}})
, then

\|f\prec g\|_{W^{s+t}}\leq C\|f\|_{B_{\infty,r_{1}}^{t}}\|D^{k}g\|_{W^{s-k}},\quad\|f\prec\!\!\!\prec g(t)\|_{W^{s+t}}\leq C\|f\|_{C_{t}\mathcal{C}^{t}}\|g\|_{W^{s}}.

•

3. If
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}\leq 1
,
\frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}\leq 1
and
s=s_{1}+s_{2}>0
, then

\|f\circ g\|_{W^{s}}\leq C\|f\|_{W^{s_{1}}}\|g\|_{W^{s_{2}}}.

For space-time mixed-type space
\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}(T)
, we have

Lemma 2.3
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,2)
,
\epsilon>0
,
\rho\in(0,\gamma)
satisfied
\gamma\leq\rho+\epsilon
. Let
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\epsilon}(T)
,
g\in W^{\alpha}(T)
and
\mathcal{L}g\in C_{T}W^{\alpha}
. Then

\displaystyle\|f\prec\!\!\!\prec g\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\alpha+\rho}(T)}\lesssim T^{1-\frac{\rho}{\gamma}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\epsilon}(T)}(\|g\|_{C_{T}W^{\alpha+\gamma-\epsilon}}+\|\mathcal{L}g\|_{C_{T}W^{\alpha}}).

.

r<\frac{1}{8\|\mathcal{B}\|}\quad with\quad\|\mathcal{B}\|=\sup_{\|u\|_{E},\|v\|_{E}\leq 1}\|\mathcal{B}(u,v)\|_{E}.

(2.7)

and some control property on

2.3
Improving Gronwall lemma

In this section, we introduce a Improving Gronwall lemma also called linear Volterra integral inequality. Firstly, we introduce the
Mittag-Leffler function

Definition 2.1
.

Let
a>0
. The function
E_{a}
define by

E_{a}(z):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{j}}{\Gamma(ja+1)}

(2.10)

whenever the series converges is called the Mittag-Leffler function of order
a
.

The convergence of the series is guaranteed by the following proposition.

Proposition 2.1
.

Consider the two-parameter Mittag-Leffler function
E_{a,b}
for some
a,b>0
. The power series defining
E_{a,b}(z)
is convergent for all
z\in\mathbb{C}
. In other words,
E_{a,b}
is an entire function.

Next, we introduce the improving Gronwall lemma, which is the conclusion in
[
DF10
]
, we give another proof.

Lemma 2.6

(Lemma 6.19 in
[
DF10
]
)
.

Let
a,T,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Moreover assume that
g(t)
is a non negative continuous function satisfying the inequality

g(t)\leq f(t)+M\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{a-1}g(s)\mathrm{d}s

(2.11)

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Then

g(t)\leq f(t)E_{a}(\Gamma(a)Mt^{a})

(2.12)

for
t\in[0,T]
.

3
Well-posedness of GFSB

Before we prove Theorem
1.2
and
1.3
, we prove two useful lemma

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
r
be
b
-order regular function on
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
satisfied
r(\tau^{*})=\alpha<0
,
\alpha+b>0
. Then for any
\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, we have
r(\tau)\geq 2\alpha+b
.

Proof.

Note
\alpha+b>0
, if
\tau=\tau^{*}
or
\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*}
or
(\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*})\cdot\tau^{*}
, then the lemma is proved. In fact
\tau=\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*}
, then
r(\tau)=2\alpha+b
. Let
\tau=(\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*})\cdot\tau^{*}
, then by a directly calculation, we have

\displaystyle r(\tau)=

\displaystyle\min(r(\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*}),r(\tau^{*}),r(\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*})+r(\tau^{*}))+b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(2\alpha+b,\alpha,3\alpha+b)+b.

If
2\alpha+b>0
, then we have
r(\tau)=\alpha+b
; If
2\alpha+b\leq 0
, then we have
r(\tau)=3\alpha+2b
. Now we assume that there exists a
\tau_{k}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
such that
r(\tau)<2\alpha+b
. By the definition of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, there exists
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\{\tau_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
such that
\tau=(\tau_{1}\cdot\tau_{2})
. By the definition of
r
, we have

\displaystyle r(\tau)=\min(r(\tau_{1}),r(\tau_{2}),r(\tau_{1})+r(\tau_{2}))+b<2\alpha+b.

We claim that at least one of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
satisfied

r(\tau_{1})\>or\>r(\tau_{2})<2\alpha+b.

In fact, assume that
r(\tau_{1})
and
r(\tau_{2})
all larger than
2\alpha+b
, then
r(\tau)
satisfied

\displaystyle r(\tau)>\min(2\alpha+b,4\alpha+2b)+b=\min(2\alpha+2b,4\alpha+3b)

note that
2\alpha+2b>2\alpha+b
and
4\alpha+3b=2\alpha+b+2(\alpha+b)>2\alpha+b
, we get contradiction which prove our claim. Then we can obtain a chain

\displaystyle\tau^{0}\to\tau^{1}\to\cdots\tau^{n}\to\cdots,\quad\tau^{m}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime},m\in\mathbb{N}.

such that
r(\tau^{m})<2\alpha+b
, and
\tau^{m}
can be written as
\tau^{m+1}\cdot\tau^{\prime}
for some
\tau^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
. The above procedure can only be implemented a finite number of times, hence there exists a longest chain which we

\displaystyle\tau^{0}\to\tau^{1}\to\cdots\tau^{N}.

It’s not difficult to check
\tau^{N}=\tau^{*}
and
\tau^{N-1}=\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*}
.
But
r(\tau^{*}\cdot\tau^{*})=2\alpha+b
, we have reached a contradiction which prove our lemma.
∎

proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Fix

is the constant in (

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(L(v))=D(v\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}c(\tau)X^{\tau}),\quad

By Lemma

\displaystyle\|\sum_{(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}}X^{\tau}X^{\tau^{\prime}}\|_{C_{T}W^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|X^{\tau}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{r(\tau)}(T)}\|X^{\tau^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{r(\tau^{\prime})}(T)}\lesssim\|\mathbb{X}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha}}^{2}.

(3.1)

Note that
0<s<\rho

X^{\tau^{*}}\in C_{T}W^{\alpha}

Finally, we prove the continuous dependence on initial data and enhance data. Let

fg-\tilde{f}\tilde{g}=(f-\tilde{f})g+\tilde{f}(g-\tilde{g})

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}(\Delta_{1,2}v)\|_{s-\gamma+\delta;t}\lesssim

\displaystyle(\|\mathbb{X}^{1}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}+\|\mathbb{X}^{2}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}})\|\Delta_{1,2}\mathbb{X}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}+\|\Delta_{1,2}\mathbb{X}^{1}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}\|v^{1}\|_{s;t}

\displaystyle+(\|\mathbb{X}^{2}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}+\|v^{1}\|_{s;t}+\|v^{2}\|_{s;t})\|\Delta_{1,2}v\|_{s;t}.

Noting that
\|\mathbb{X}^{1}\|_{X_{t}^{\alpha,b}}

, for

. Recall the estimate

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{1,2}v\|_{s;t}\leq

\displaystyle+\|\Delta_{1,2}\mathbb{X}^{1}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}\|v^{1}\|_{s;T}t^{\frac{\delta}{\gamma}}+(\|\mathbb{X}^{2}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}+\|v^{1}\|_{s;T}+\|v^{2}\|_{s;T})\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{\frac{\delta}{\gamma}-1}\|\Delta_{1,2}v\|_{s;\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau

. By Lemma
2.6
, for any

, we have

.
∎

\|u^{1}\|_{s,t}\leq CM
, Note that

Note that

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\Delta_{1,2}h(u^{2,\sharp})+\mathcal{B}(\Delta_{1,2}u^{\mathcal{Q}},u^{1,\mathcal{Q}_{1}})+\mathcal{B}(u^{2,\mathcal{Q}_{2}},\Delta_{1,2}u^{\mathcal{Q}})+\mathcal{L}\mathcal{G}_{1}(\Delta_{1,2}u^{\mathcal{Q}})+\mathcal{L}\Delta_{1,2}\mathcal{G}(u^{2,\mathcal{Q}_{2}}).

Using the similar estimate above, for
t\leq{T^{*}}
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}(\Delta_{1,2}u^{\sharp})\|_{2s-\gamma+\delta;t}\lesssim

for some finite

.

Next, we obtain the solution until blow up time. Similar as the proof of Theorem
1.2
, assume that we already have the solution
(u^{\prime},u^{\sharp})
on
[0,h]
for some
h>0
, and consider the time interval
[h,h+1]
. The initial condition has enough regularity by following computation

\displaystyle\tilde{u}^{\sharp}(0)=

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(0)-\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}c(\tau)\tilde{X}^{\tau}-\tilde{u}^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u(h)-\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}c(\tau)X^{\tau}(h)-u^{\prime}(h)\prec\mathcal{Q}(h)

Note that
u^{\prime}\prec\mathcal{Q}(\tau)-u^{\prime}\prec\mathcal{Q}(\tau)\in W^{2s}
, we have
\tilde{u}^{\sharp}(0)\in W^{2s}
. As a consequence, let
\tilde{M}=C(\|\tilde{u}^{\sharp}(0)\|_{W^{2s}})
, then we obtain unique paracontrolled solution
\tilde{u}
on
[0,h(\tilde{M})]
. We then extend
u
and
u^{\prime}
from
[0,\tau]
to
[0,h+h(\tilde{M})]
by setting
u(t)=\tilde{u}(t-h)
for
t\geq h
. Then by definition
u^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}(h+h(\tilde{M}))
. For the remainder term, note that
u^{\mathcal{Q}}-u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec_{h}\mathcal{Q}\in W^{2s}
where

\displaystyle u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec_{\tau}\mathcal{Q}(t)=\sum_{j}\int_{-\infty}^{t}2^{\gamma j}\varphi(2^{\gamma j}(t-s))S_{j-1}u^{\prime}(s\vee h)\mathrm{d}s\Delta_{j}\mathcal{Q}(t).

It suffice to show that
(u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec_{h}\mathcal{Q}-u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q})|_{[h,h+h(\tilde{M})]}\in\mathcal{W}^{2s}([h,h+h(\tilde{M})])
.

Noting that
\tilde{u}
is the solution on
[0,h(\tilde{M})]
and the fact

\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}\|_{s;t}\leq c\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\prime}\|_{s;t}+\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\sharp}\|_{2s;t}+\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}\|\tilde{u}^{1}\|_{s;t}.

(3.8)

on
t\in[0,h(\tilde{M})]
. Using the similar argument on
[0,h(M)]
, we also have the estimate

\displaystyle c\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\prime}\|_{s;t}+\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\sharp}\|_{2s;t}\leq\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\|_{X_{T}^{\alpha,b}}f(t)E_{a}(\Gamma(a)Nt^{a}).

noting that
\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\prime}\|_{s;t}=\|\Delta_{1,2}u^{\prime}\|_{s;(h(M),h(M)+t)}
,
\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\sharp}\|_{2s;t}=\|\Delta_{1,2}u^{\sharp}\|_{2s;(h(M),h(M)+t)}
. For
t\in[0,h(M)+h(\tilde{M})]
, we have

\displaystyle c\|\Delta_{1,2}u^{\prime}\|_{s;t}+\|\Delta_{1,2}u^{\sharp}\|_{2s;t}\leq

\displaystyle\max(c\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\prime}\|_{s;t-h(M)\vee 0}+\|\Delta_{1,2}u^{\sharp}\|_{2s;t-h(M)\vee 0}

\displaystyle,c\|\Delta_{1,2}\tilde{u}^{\prime}\|_{s;t\wedge h(M)}+\|\Delta_{1,2}u^{\sharp}\|_{2s;t\wedge h(M)}).

Let
M=M^{0}
and
\tilde{M}=M^{1}
, iterated such argument to
M^{n}
, we can prove

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u^{\prime}\|_{s;h(M^{n})}+2\|u^{\sharp}\|_{2s;h(M^{n})}=\infty,

by contradiction. Then we obtain the solution until blow up time with uniqueness and continuous dependence with initial data.
Then by our assumption, we finish our proof.
∎

4
Driving by
|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi

In this section, we restrict
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
and
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
. Let us beginning with (
1.5
), To define it completely, choosing a function
\phi:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
, that is even, smooth, compactly supported, decreasing on

, and such that
\phi(0)=1
, and then setting

\xi_{\epsilon}(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi(\epsilon k)\mathcal{F}(\xi)(k))(x),\quad u^{\epsilon}_{0}(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi(\epsilon k)\mathcal{F}(u_{0})(k))(x).

Its obviously that

is smooth by distribution theory. Then (
1.5
) is a smooth equation. Recalling equation (
1.6
) for
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
as follows

\partial_{t}Y_{\epsilon}-\Lambda^{\gamma}Y_{\epsilon}=|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{\epsilon},

(4.1)

denoting

, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}=\frac{1}{2}D(Y_{\epsilon}^{2}+(u^{(1)}_{\epsilon})^{2}+2Y_{\epsilon}u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}),\quad u^{(1)}_{\epsilon}=u_{0}^{\epsilon}

It can be solved by similar argument in

]

]

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a finite index set, and let
\{X_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{T}}
be a a collection of real or complex-valued centred jointly Gaussian random variables. Then,

\mathbb{E}\prod_{\alpha\in\mathcal{T}}X_{\alpha}=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T})}\prod_{\{\alpha,\beta\}\in P}\mathbb{E}[X_{\alpha}X_{\beta}].

(4.4)

. By Duhanmel formula, we have
Y_{\epsilon,k}^{\hbox to7.99pt{\vbox to4.19pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.99448pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.04448pt}{3.04448pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{3.04448pt}{3.04448pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(t)=0
for
k=0
and

Y^{\hbox to7.99pt{\vbox to4.19pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.99448pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L -4.21 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.17 4.21 C -3.17 4.79 -3.64 5.25 -4.21 5.25 C -4.79 5.25 -5.25 4.79 -5.25 4.21 C -5.25 3.64 -4.79 3.17 -4.21 3.17 C -3.64 3.17 -3.17 3.64 -3.17 4.21 Z M -4.21 4.21}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.04448pt}{3.04448pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 4.21 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.25 4.21 C 5.25 4.79 4.79 5.25 4.21 5.25 C 3.64 5.25 3.17 4.79 3.17 4.21 C 3.17 3.64 3.64 3.17 4.21 3.17 C 4.79 3.17 5.25 3.64 5.25 4.21 Z M 4.21 4.21}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{3.04448pt}{3.04448pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}\par
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}_{\epsilon,k}(t)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t}|k|e^{-(t-\tau)|k|^{\gamma}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\phi(\epsilon j)\phi(\epsilon(k-j))Y_{j}Y_{k-j}\mathrm{d}\tau,

(4.5)

for
k\neq 0
. Fix

\displaystyle f_{k,j}(t):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t}|k|e^{-(t-\tau)|k|^{\gamma}}Y_{j}Y_{k-j}\mathrm{d}\tau,

we consider the estimate of
F(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(t,s):=\mathbb{E}[f_{k,j}(t)f_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(s)]
and
\hat{F}(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(t,s)=\mathbb{E}[(f_{k,j}(t)-f_{k,j}(s))(f_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(t)-f_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(s))]
. For the first, we have

\displaystyle F(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(t,s)=\frac{1}{4}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{s}|k||k^{\prime}|e^{-(t-\tau)|k|^{\gamma}}e^{-(s-r)|k^{\prime}|^{\gamma}}(S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3})\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}r,

where

\displaystyle S_{1}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}(\tau)Y_{k-j}(\tau)]\mathbb{E}[Y_{j^{\prime}}(r)Y_{k^{\prime}-j^{\prime}}(r)],

\displaystyle S_{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}(\tau)Y_{k^{\prime}-j^{\prime}}(r)]\mathbb{E}[Y_{j^{\prime}}(r)Y_{k-j}(\tau)],

\displaystyle S_{3}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}(\tau)Y_{j^{\prime}}(r)]\mathbb{E}[Y_{k^{\prime}-j^{\prime}}(r)Y_{k-j}(\tau)].

Define
\delta_{\kappa,\eta}=1
for
\kappa=\eta
and
\delta_{\kappa,\eta}=0
for
\kappa\neq\eta
, we have that

\displaystyle|j|^{\gamma-1}|j^{\prime}|^{\gamma-1}S_{1}=

\displaystyle 2\pi 1_{j=j-k}1_{j^{\prime}=j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}},

\displaystyle|j|^{\gamma-1}|k-j|^{\gamma-1}S_{2}=

\displaystyle 2\pi e^{-|\tau-r||j|^{\gamma}}e^{-|\tau-r||k-j|^{\gamma}}1_{j=j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}1_{j^{\prime}=j-k},

\displaystyle|j|^{\gamma-1}|k-j|^{\gamma-1}S_{3}=

\displaystyle 2\pi e^{-|\tau-r||j|^{\gamma}}e^{-|\tau-r||k-j|^{\gamma}}1_{j=-j^{\prime}}1_{k^{\prime}-j^{\prime}=j-k}.

We have the first term is zero. The remain two parts always lead that
k^{\prime}=-k
, we estimate that

\displaystyle\delta_{k,k^{\prime}}(\delta_{j,j^{\prime}+k}+\delta_{j,-j^{\prime}})\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{s}e^{-(t-\tau)|k|^{\gamma}-(s-r)|k|^{\gamma}-|\tau-r||j|^{\gamma}-|\tau-r||k-j|^{\gamma}}\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{k,k^{\prime}}(\delta_{j,j^{\prime}+k}+\delta_{j,-j^{\prime}})(\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-(t-s-\tau-r)|k|^{\gamma}-|\tau-r||j|^{\gamma}-|\tau-r||k-j|^{\gamma}}\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t-s}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-(t-s-\tau-r)|k|^{\gamma}-|\tau-r||j|^{\gamma}-|\tau-r||k-j|^{\gamma}}\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{k,k^{\prime}}(\delta_{j,j^{\prime}+k}+\delta_{j,-j^{\prime}})(\frac{e^{-|j|^{\gamma}|t-s|-|k-j|^{\gamma}|t-s|}-e^{-|k|^{\gamma}|t-s|}}{(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})(|k|^{\gamma}-|j|^{\gamma}-|k-j|^{\gamma})}

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{-|k|^{\gamma}|t-s|}}{|k|^{\gamma}(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})}),

where for the first integral, we use the symmetric for the kernel with
s
and
t
, and second use
r>\tau
. Then by Lemma
4.1
and
\gamma>\frac{3}{2}
, we can check there exist
a>0
, such that the first convergence condition

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\sup_{[0,T]}|F_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(t,t)||k|^{a}|k^{\prime}|^{a}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}}\sup_{[0,T]}\frac{|k|^{4-2\gamma}}{|k|^{\gamma}(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})}|k|^{a}|k|^{a}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}}\sup_{[0,T]}\frac{|k|^{4-2\gamma}}{|k|^{\gamma}(|k|^{\frac{\gamma}{3}}|j|^{\frac{\gamma}{3}}|k-j|^{\frac{\gamma}{3}})}|k|^{a}|k|^{a}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}}|k|^{4-\frac{10}{3}\gamma+2a}<\infty.

Now we focus on the covariance which is vital for the regularity, a simple calculation that

\hat{F}(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(t,s)=F(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(t,t)+F(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(s,s)-F(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(t,s)-F(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(s,t).

(4.6)

and notice that
|\frac{1-e^{-x}}{|x|^{\delta}}|
is finite for any
\delta\in[0,1]
, by Corollary
B.1
we estimate the integral

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\frac{2}{|k|^{\gamma}(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})}-\frac{2e^{-|j|^{\gamma}|t-s|-|k-j|^{\gamma}|t-s|}-2e^{-|k|^{\gamma}|t-s|}}{(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})(|k|^{\gamma}-|j|^{\gamma}-|k-j|^{\gamma})}

\displaystyle-\frac{2e^{-|k|^{\gamma}(t-s)}}{|k|^{\gamma}(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})}]|k|^{2}|k-j|^{1-\gamma}|j|^{1-\gamma},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2(|k|^{\gamma}-|j|^{\gamma}-|k-j|^{\gamma})+2(|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})e^{-|k|^{\gamma}|t-s|}-2|k|^{\gamma}e^{-(|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})|t-s|}}{(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})(|k|^{\gamma}-|j|^{\gamma}-|k-j|^{\gamma})|k|^{\gamma-2}|k-j|^{\gamma-1}|j|^{\gamma-1}},

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{(C\wedge 2(|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})|k|^{\gamma}|t-s|^{2})}{(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})|k|^{\gamma}|k-j|^{\gamma-1}|j|^{\gamma-1}},

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|t-s|^{2\delta}\frac{|k|^{\gamma\delta}(|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})^{\delta}}{(|k|^{\gamma}+|j|^{\gamma}+|k-j|^{\gamma})|k|^{\gamma}}\lesssim|t-s|^{2\delta}\frac{1}{(|k|^{\gamma(1-\delta)}+|j|^{\gamma(1-\delta)})|k|^{\gamma(1-\delta)}}.

where we use the fact
|1-e^{-ax}|\lesssim a^{\delta}x^{\delta}
for any
\delta\in(0,1]
and
a,x>0
. Since
\gamma>\frac{3}{2}
, there exist
\beta
small enough such that the second convergence condition

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|\hat{F}_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}(t,s)|}{|t-s|^{2\beta}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|k|^{4-2\gamma}}{(|k|^{\gamma(1-\beta)}+|j|^{\gamma(1-\beta)})|k|^{\gamma(1-\beta)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|k|^{4-2\gamma}}{(|k|^{\frac{\gamma}{3}(1-\beta)}|j|^{\frac{2\gamma}{3}(1-\beta)})|k|^{\gamma(1-\beta)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|k|^{4-(\frac{10}{3}-\frac{4}{3}\beta)\gamma}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{|j|^{1+}}<\infty.

By Proposition
A.1
, we proof our claim.
∎

The main strategy is similar as the above argument which use the convergence theorem
A.1
but more complex. For our convenience, we introduce some notation.

Fix
\mathbb{Z}_{*}=\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}
, and fix
L=(k,l,m)\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}^{3}
,
C_{L}(\epsilon)=\phi(\epsilon k)\phi(\epsilon l)\phi(\epsilon m)
. Define the integral operator
\mathcal{I}_{L,L^{\prime}}
as

\mathcal{I}_{L,L^{\prime}}f(t,t^{\prime})=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-|\psi(L^{\prime})|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})}|\psi(L)||\psi(L^{\prime})|f(s,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s

(4.8)

and define the tensor product as
f\otimes g(t,t^{\prime})=f(t)g(t^{\prime})
. Its obviously
H(k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime})(t,t^{\prime})
can be written as
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{LL^{\prime}}[Y_{l}Y_{m}\otimes Y_{l^{\prime}}Y_{m^{\prime}}](t,t^{\prime})]
. Similarly, rewrite

and fix

, we have the representation

where

and

is the tail of

.

Proposition 4.6
.

{{}{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -4.21 4.21 L 0 8.43}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.04 8.43 C 1.04 9 0.57 9.46 0 9.46 C -0.57 9.46 -1.04 9 -1.04 8.43 C -1.04 7.85 -0.57 7.39 0 7.39 C 0.57 7.39 1.04 7.85 1.04 8.43 Z M 0 8.43}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
as
\epsilon\to 0
in
L^{2}(\Omega;C_{T}(\mathcal{C}^{\beta}\cap H^{\beta})\cap C_{T}^{\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}L^{2})
.

Proof.

It is not difficult to observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[[\mathcal{I}_{L,L^{\prime}}f(t,t^{\prime})]=\mathcal{I}_{L,L^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}f)(t,t^{\prime})

\displaystyle Y_{1}\otimes Y_{2}(t,t^{\prime})\cdot Y_{3}\otimes Y_{4}(s,s^{\prime})=Y_{1}\otimes Y_{3}(t,s)\cdot Y_{2}\otimes Y_{4}(t^{\prime},s^{\prime})=Y_{1}\otimes Y_{4}(t,s^{\prime})\cdot Y_{2}\otimes Y_{3}(t^{\prime},s)

Then it suffice to estimate
\mathbb{E}\bigg(Y_{k}\otimes Y_{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{I}_{L_{\downarrow},L^{\prime}_{\downarrow}}((Y_{l}Y_{m})\otimes(Y_{l^{\prime}}Y_{m^{\prime}}))(t,t^{\prime})\bigg)
, it can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-|l+m|^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-|l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}[Y_{k}(t)Y_{l}(s)Y_{m}(s)Y_{k^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})Y_{l^{\prime}}(s^{\prime})Y_{m^{\prime}}(s^{\prime})]

use the proof in Proposition
B.2
, we obtain that the above integral has three parts

\displaystyle R_{L,L^{\prime}}(t,t^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}R_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{i};t,t^{\prime}).

For
H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{1};T,T^{\prime})
, we have the fact that
|\psi(L)|=|\psi(L^{\prime})|=|k+l+m|
, by Lemma
B.1
and proof in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle|H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{1};T,T^{\prime})|=

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{L,L^{\prime}}R_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{1};T,T^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|k+l+m|^{\gamma-2}(|k+l+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})}\cdot\frac{|(l+m)|^{2}|k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}}{|l+m|^{\gamma}(|l+m|^{\gamma}+(|l|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}))}

and

\displaystyle|\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{1};T,T^{\prime})|\leq\frac{1\wedge|k+l+m|^{\gamma}|T-T^{\prime}|}{|k+l+m|^{\gamma-2}(|k+l+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})}\frac{|(l+m)|^{2}|k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}}{|l+m|^{\gamma}(|l+m|^{\gamma}+(|l|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}))}

then since
l
and
m
is symmetric, we assume that
|l|\geq|m|
, for any
\overline{T}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{L\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{*}}

\displaystyle|\psi(L)|^{\frac{1}{2}+2\epsilon}\sup_{0<T\leq\overline{T}}|H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{1};T,T)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{L\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{*}}\frac{1}{|k+l+m|^{\gamma-\frac{5}{2}+\gamma\theta-2\epsilon}|k|^{\gamma-1}}\cdot\frac{|(l+m)|^{2}}{|l+m|^{\gamma(4-\theta-\bar{\theta})}|m|^{(2+\bar{\theta})\gamma-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{l,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{|(l+m)|^{2}}{|l+m|^{\gamma(4-\theta-\bar{\theta})}|m|^{(2+\bar{\theta})\gamma-2}}<\infty

for
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
, where we let
\theta,\bar{\theta}<1
but close enough to
1
and
\epsilon
small enough.

The second inequality we use the following Lemma

Lemma 4.1
.

\sum_{k\neq\{a,0\}}\frac{1}{|k-a|^{\frac{1}{2}+}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}}<\infty
uniformly for
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}
.

Proof.

Fix
R=\max(|a|,1)
. Define two sets
A=\{k:|k|\leq 2R,k\neq 0,a\}
and
B=\{k:|k|>2R\}
. On the domain
B
, we have
|k-a|\geq|k|-|a|\geq|k|-R\geq\frac{|k|}{2}
, then the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in B}\frac{1}{|k-a|^{\frac{1}{2}+}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\sum_{k\neq 0}\frac{1}{(|k|/2)^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}}<\infty

On the domain
A
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in A}\frac{1}{|k-a|^{\frac{1}{2}+}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in A,|k|\leq|k-a|}\frac{1}{|k-a|^{\frac{1}{2}+}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\sum_{k\in A,|k|>|k-a|}\frac{1}{|k-a|^{\frac{1}{2}+}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq 0}\frac{1}{|k|^{1+}}+\sum_{k\neq{a}}\frac{1}{|k-a|^{1+}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq 0}\frac{1}{|k|^{1+}}+\sum_{k^{\prime}\neq{0}}\frac{1}{|k^{\prime}|^{1+}}.

We have two sums, each possessing an upper bound independent of
a
, therefore the upper bound for the original sum is also independent of
a
.
∎

Similar as above argument, for
\beta\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma}+{\frac{\epsilon}{\gamma}}
and any
\overline{T}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{L\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{*}}\sup_{0<T,T^{\prime}\leq\overline{T}}\frac{|\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{1};T,T^{\prime})|}{|T-T^{\prime}|^{2\beta}}<\infty.

For
H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{2};T,T^{\prime})
, the fact that
|\psi(L)|=|\psi(L^{\prime})|=|k+l+m|
is also valid, then by Lemma
B.1
and proof in Proposition
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{L,L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{*}}

\displaystyle|\psi(L)|^{\frac{1}{2}+2\epsilon}\sup_{0<T\leq\overline{T}}|H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{2};T,T^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{L\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{*}}\frac{1}{|k+l+m|^{(1+\theta)\gamma-\frac{5}{2}-2\epsilon}(|k|^{\gamma}\wedge|l|^{\gamma})^{\gamma(1-\theta)}}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{1}{|l+m|^{\gamma\bar{\theta}-1}|k+m|^{\gamma\bar{\theta}-1}|l|^{\gamma(2-\bar{\theta})-1}|k|^{\gamma(2-\bar{\theta})-1}|m|^{\gamma-1}}

Since
l
and
k
is symmetric, without loss of generality, assume that
|k|\geq|l|
, by Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{L,L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{*}}

\displaystyle|\psi(L)|^{\frac{1}{2}+2\epsilon}\sup_{0<T\leq\overline{T}}|H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{2};T,T^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{1}{|l|^{\gamma(1-\theta)}}\cdot\frac{1}{|l+m|^{\gamma\bar{\theta}-1}|l|^{2\gamma(2-\bar{\theta})-2}|m|^{\gamma-1}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{1}{|k+l+m|^{(1+\theta)\gamma-\frac{5}{2}-2\epsilon}|k+m|^{\gamma\bar{\theta}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{1}{|l|^{\gamma(1-\theta)}|l|^{2\gamma(2-\bar{\theta})-2}}\cdot\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{1}{|l+m|^{\gamma\bar{\theta}-1}|m|^{\gamma-1}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{1}{|k+l+m|^{(1+\theta)\gamma-\frac{5}{2}-2\epsilon}|k+m|^{\gamma\bar{\theta}-1}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

for
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
, where we fix
\theta,\bar{\theta}
be closed enough to
1
and
\epsilon
small enough. Similarly, by Lemma
B.1
, for
\beta<\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{\epsilon}{\gamma}
and any
\overline{T}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{L\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{*}}\sup_{0<T,T^{\prime}\leq\overline{T}}\frac{\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{2};T,T^{\prime})}{|T-T^{\prime}|^{2\beta}}<\infty.

For
H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};T,T^{\prime})
, under the pairing
P^{3}
, we have
|\psi(L)|=|\psi(L^{\prime})|=|m|
is independent of
k,k^{\prime}
, Lemma
B.2
lead that for any
\beta<3\gamma-\frac{7}{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{L,L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}^{3}}1_{\psi(L_{\downarrow})\neq 0}

\displaystyle|\psi(L)|^{2\beta}\sup_{0<T\leq\overline{T}}H_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};T,T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}|m|^{2\beta}\mathcal{I}_{m,m^{\prime}}\sum_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}R_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};T,T)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{|m|^{2\beta}}{|m|^{6\gamma-8}}<\infty

for some small enough
\epsilon
and
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{2},2]
. Similar as above argument, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{L,L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}^{3}}

\displaystyle\sup_{0<T,T^{\prime}\leq\overline{T}}\frac{\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};T,T^{\prime})}{|T-T^{\prime}|^{2\beta}}<\infty.

Summary above all, by Proposition
A.1
, we prove our claim.
∎

. Before we prove our result, we present a lemma to simplify the difference of the equations and note that this approximation should be a natural consequence of well-posedness.

Proposition 4.8
.

For
\gamma>\frac{3}{2}
and
u_{0}\in W^{\frac{1}{4}+\delta}
, There exists
T
and enhance data
\mathbb{X}\in X^{-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}
for some
\delta>0
, such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(\|(u-u_{\epsilon},v-v_{\epsilon})\|_{C_{T}W^{-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}\times C_{T}W^{\frac{1}{4}+\delta}}>\rho)

\displaystyle=0

on
[0,T]
for any
\rho>0
. where
u
is the solution of (
1.11
) driven by
(\mathbb{X},u_{0})
. Moreover, there exists
T
, enhance data
\mathbb{X}
,
I,\mathcal{J},\mathcal{K},\mathcal{M}
and
\tilde{X}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(\|(u-u_{\epsilon},u^{\prime}-u_{\epsilon}^{\prime},u^{\sharp}-u^{\sharp}_{\epsilon})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}\times\mathcal{W}^{\frac{1}{4}+\delta}\times\mathcal{W}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}>\rho)

\displaystyle=0

for any
\rho>0
.

By (
1.16
), we have

c\|u^{\prime}-u^{\prime}_{\epsilon}\|+\|u^{\sharp}-u^{\sharp}_{\epsilon}\|\leq

(4.14)

∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Fix
a_{\epsilon}(\omega),b(\omega)>0
, satisfied
a_{\epsilon}(\omega)\to 0
in
L^{2}(\Omega)
and
b(\omega)<\infty
a.s, then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(a_{\epsilon}b>\delta)=0

for any
\delta>0
.

Proof.

Fix
\delta>0
, then there exists
N
such that
\mathbb{P}(b\geq N)<\delta^{2}
, by Chebyshev’s Inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a_{\epsilon}b>\delta)\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a_{\epsilon}b>\delta,b=N)\mathbb{P}(b\geq N)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a_{\epsilon}>\frac{\delta}{N})\mathbb{P}(b\geq N)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[|a_{\epsilon}|^{2}]}{(\delta/N)^{2}}\delta^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{2}\mathbb{E}[|a_{\epsilon}|^{2}]\to 0,\quad as\>\epsilon\to 0.

the claim is proved.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

By Proposition
4.8
, we have that
u_{\epsilon}\to u
in
C_{T}W^{-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}
for some
\delta
, and by Theorem
1.3
or Theorem
1.2
,
u
has a structure of
\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}c(\tau)X^{\tau}+v
, where
v
is a high regularity term, then we finish the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
∎

. Noting that
u^{\mathcal{Q}}=u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q}+u^{\sharp}
which will not behave better than
\mathcal{Q}
(by Schauder estimate
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{W}^{\frac{1}{4}-}(T)
), we can not simply define
\mathcal{J}(u^{\mathcal{Q}},X^{\tau^{*}})
as
\partial_{x}(u^{\mathcal{Q}}\circ X^{\tau^{*}})+\partial_{x}(X^{\tau^{*}}\prec u^{\mathcal{Q}})
. A useful method is to decompose it into following two parts

u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}=((u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q}-u^{\prime}\prec\mathcal{Q})\circ X^{\tau^{*}})+(u^{\prime}\prec\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}-u^{\prime}\mathcal{Q}\circ X)+u^{\prime}\cdot Q\circ X^{\tau^{*}}

(5.1)

where the first term on the right hand side of (
5.1
) is high order term by commutator estimate, and the second term is well-defined by para-controlled calculus, and the third term need the stochastic cancellation if we fix
X^{\tau^{*}}
satisfying .
Now we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(X^{\tau_{m}},X^{\tau^{*}})=

\displaystyle[X^{\tau_{m}}\prec\partial_{x}X^{\tau^{*}}-X^{\tau_{m}}\prec\!\!\!\prec\partial_{x}X^{\tau^{*}}]+[X^{\tau_{m}}\prec\!\!\!\prec\partial_{x}X^{\tau^{*}}-\mathcal{L}(X^{\tau_{m}}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q})]+\partial_{x}(X^{\tau^{*}}\prec X^{\tau_{m}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(u^{\mathcal{Q}},X^{\tau^{*}})=

\displaystyle\partial_{x}(X^{\tau^{*}}\prec u^{\mathcal{Q}}),\quad\mathcal{K}(u^{\prime},\mathcal{Q})=(u^{\prime}\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}-u^{\prime}\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(u^{\sharp},X^{\tau^{*}})=

\displaystyle u^{\sharp}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}\quad\tilde{X}=\mathcal{Q}\circ X^{\tau^{*}}.

By Theorem, we can get the there exists
(u^{\prime},u^{\sharp})
satisfying . Finally we prove the solution is the generalized solution of following equation

\partial_{t}u-\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}}u=\partial_{x}(u^{2})+|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi

(5.2)

it only need a simple calculation, or finding the similar representation in
[
GP17
]
.

Appendix A
Gaussian computation

In this section, we give some useful computations which take crucial rules in computation of high order expansion.

Proposition A.1
.

Fix
\mathcal{Z}
be a countable index set, and
\{f_{\kappa}\}_{\kappa\in\mathcal{Z}}
be a family of continuous stochastic process belonging to
\mathcal{I}_{k}
for some fixed value
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and write

F_{\kappa\eta}(t)=\mathbb{E}f_{\kappa}(t)f_{\eta}(t),\quad\hat{F}_{\kappa\eta}(s,t)=\mathbb{E}[(f_{\kappa}(t)-f_{\kappa}(s))(f_{\eta}(t)-f_{\eta}(s))].

We also assume that
f_{i\kappa}=\bar{f}_{\kappa}
. Let
\{C_{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon\in(0,1]}
be a family of functions
C_{\epsilon}:\mathcal{Z}\to[0,1]
such that

•

C_{\epsilon}(\kappa)>C_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\kappa)
for
\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}
;

•

Set
\{\kappa:C_{\epsilon}(\kappa)\neq 0\}
is finite for every
\epsilon>0
,

•

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}C_{\epsilon}(\kappa)=1
for every
\kappa\in\mathcal{Z}
.

Finally,
g_{\kappa}(x)
be a family of Lipschitz continuous functions such that

•

\mathcal{F}(g_{\kappa}(x))(k)
is a constant when
k=\psi(\kappa)
and zero otherwise, where
\psi:\mathcal{Z}\mapsto\mathbb{Z}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.

•

there is a involution
\iota:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Z}
such that
g_{\iota\kappa}=\bar{g}_{\kappa}
and
\psi(\iota\kappa)=-k

.

Let
F_{\epsilon}
be the stochastic process defined by

F_{\epsilon}(x,t)=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{Z}}C_{\epsilon}(\kappa)f_{\kappa}(t)g_{\kappa}(x)

(A.1)

where
g_{\kappa}(x)
satisfied Then there exist a process
F
satisfied
F_{\epsilon}\to F
in
L^{2}(\Omega;C_{T}H_{p}^{\alpha})
if following condition satisfied
k\neq\eta
,

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{\kappa,\eta}(s,t)=0,\quad\psi(\kappa)\neq\psi(\eta)

(A.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa,\eta\in\mathcal{Z}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|F_{\kappa\eta}(t)||\psi(\kappa)|^{b}|\psi(\eta)|^{b}<\infty

(A.3)

\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa,\eta\in\mathcal{Z}}\sup_{s,t\in[0,T]}|\frac{\hat{F}_{\kappa\eta}(s,t)}{|t-s|^{\alpha}}<\infty

(A.4)

then for any
p\geq 1
, there exist
F_{p}
such that
F_{\epsilon}\to F_{p}
in
L^{2}(\Omega;H_{p}^{\alpha})
.

Proof.

Define
F^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(x,t):=F_{\epsilon}(x,t)-F_{\bar{\epsilon}}(x,t)
, we first show that
\mathbb{E}\|F^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}\|_{C_{T}H^{\alpha}}^{2}\to 0
when
\bar{\epsilon}<\epsilon\to 0
. By the definition of
H^{\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|F^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}}^{2}]=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}(|k|^{2})^{\alpha}|\mathcal{F}(F^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon})(k)|^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}(|k|^{2})^{\alpha}\sum_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}(C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa)C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa^{\prime}))\sup_{t\in[0,T]}F_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}(t)\mathcal{F}(g_{\kappa})(k)\mathcal{F}(g_{\iota\kappa^{\prime}})(-k)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|k|^{2\alpha}\sum_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}(C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa)C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa^{\prime}))\sup_{t\in[0,T]}F_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}(t)\textbf{1}_{\psi(\kappa)=\psi(\kappa)=k}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}|C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa)C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa^{\prime})|\sum_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}|\psi(\kappa)|^{2a}|\psi(\kappa^{\prime})|^{\alpha}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}F_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}(t)\to 0

when
\bar{\epsilon}<\epsilon\to 0
. On the other hand, to prove
F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}
, by Parsavel formula, we estimate that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|F^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(x,t)-F^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(x,s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2p}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|\mathcal{F}(F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(t)-F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(s))(k)|^{2})^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathcal{F}(F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(t)-F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(s))(k_{i})|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}(\mathbb{E}\mathcal{F}(F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(t)-F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(s))(k_{i})|^{2p})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{F}(F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(t)-F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(s))(k_{i})|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\hat{F}_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}(s,t)\textbf{1}_{\psi(\kappa)=\psi(\kappa)=k}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\sup_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}|C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa)C^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\epsilon}(\kappa^{\prime}))^{p}||t-s|^{\alpha p}

choose
p
large enough, and let
\epsilon\to 0
, then we can get for
\beta<a
, by a modified version of Kolmogorov’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\|F_{\epsilon}^{\bar{\epsilon}}\|_{C^{\beta}_{T}L^{2}}\to 0.

there exist
F\in C_{T}H^{b}\cap C_{T}^{a}L^{2}
such that
F_{\epsilon}\to F
in
C_{T}H^{b}\cap C_{T}^{a}L^{2}
.

To prove that
F
belongs to
C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{b}\cap C^{a}\mathcal{C}
, by Lemma A.1 in
[
Hai13
]
, it suffice to show that
g_{\kappa}(x)
satisfies following two properties

\|g_{\kappa}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq 1,\quad\|g_{\kappa}(x)\|\leq\psi(\kappa)

(A.5)

for some
\psi(\kappa)\geq 1
.
∎

Remark A.1
.

If
F_{\epsilon}
without zero-frequency, we also have the non-homogeneous result
F_{\epsilon}\in H^{\alpha}
.

Appendix B
Other useful estimate

In this section, we introduce some useful estimates build in
[
Hai13
]
and supplement some we need.
If
|\tau-r|
and
\tau^{\prime}-r^{\prime}
has different weight in above Proposition, we have following modification with additional condition.

Proposition B.1
.

[
[
Hai13
]
]

•

(1) The bound

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\tau}\int_{-\infty}^{\tau^{\prime}}e^{-a|\tau-r|-a|\tau^{\prime}-r^{\prime}|-b|r-r^{\prime}|}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r=

\displaystyle\frac{ae^{-b|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|}-be^{-a|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|}}{a(a^{2}-b^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a(a+b)}\wedge\frac{e^{-(a\wedge b)|t-t^{\prime}|}}{a|a-b|}.

hold for
a,b>0
,
\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.

•

(2)The bound

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\exp(-a|s-s^{\prime}|-b|t-s|-c|t^{\prime}-s^{\prime}|-d|t^{\prime}-s|-e|t-s^{\prime}|)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{10e^{-(d\wedge e)|t-t^{\prime}|}}{(b+d)(c+e)+a((b+d)\wedge(c+e))}

hold for every
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
and every
a,b,c,d,e>0
.

•

(3) For every
s<t
and
u,v>0
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}e^{-u|x-s|-v|x-t|}\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{4}{u+v}

•

(4) For
a,b,t>0
, we bound that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{be^{-at}-ae^{-b(t)}}{b-a}-1\bigg|\leq 2abt^{2}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}

Moreover, if
K
satisfied
\sup_{y\geq 0}|f(y)-yf^{\prime}(y)|\leq K
, then

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{be^{-at}-ae^{-b(t)}}{b-a}\bigg|\leq K

Corollary B.1
.

For
a,b,t>0
, we bound that

\displaystyle\bigg|be^{-at}-ae^{-b(t)}-(b-a)\bigg|\leq(C\wedge 2abt^{2}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty})|b-a|

for some
C>1

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
a>b
, then we have bound

\displaystyle|be^{-at}-ae^{-b(t)}|\leq|b-a|e^{-at}+|b-a|ate^{-at}\leq C|b-a|.

Then the claim is followed by Proposition
B.1
.
∎

Lemma B.1
.

Let
H_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}_{L,L^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}(T,T^{\prime})
, where the operator
\mathcal{I}_{L,L^{\prime}}
is define as (
4.8
)
\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})
has the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})|\leq Fe^{-a|t-t^{\prime}|}

where
a,b,c>0
. Fix
\hat{H}(T,T^{\prime})=H(T,T)+H(T^{\prime},T^{\prime})-H(T,T^{\prime})-H(T^{\prime},T)
, if
|\psi(L)|=|\psi(L^{\prime})|
, then
\hat{H}
and
H
has estimate

\displaystyle|H_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}+a)}\wedge\frac{e^{-(|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}\wedge a)|T-T^{\prime}|}}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}||\psi(L)|^{\gamma}-a|}\cdot F

\displaystyle|\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle F\frac{1\wedge|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}|T-T^{\prime}|}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}+a)}

Proof.

With our loss of generality, we assume that
T\geq T^{\prime}
. Rewrite
H_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})
as follows

\displaystyle H_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{-\infty}^{T^{\prime}}e^{(-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(T+T^{\prime}-t-t^{\prime}))}\mathcal{K}(t-t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T-T^{\prime}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(T-T^{\prime}-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t-t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\delta}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(\delta-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t-t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle H(\delta),\quad\delta=T-T^{\prime}

By Proposition
B.1
, we have

\displaystyle|H(\delta)|\leq\frac{1}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}+a)}\wedge\frac{e^{-(|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}\wedge a)\delta}}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}||\psi(L)|^{\gamma}-a|}\cdot F.

since
T,T^{\prime}
is symmetric, we obtain our first result.

For
\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})
, we calculate that

\displaystyle|\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})|=

\displaystyle|2H_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})-H_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T)-\hat{H}_{L,L^{\prime}}(T^{\prime},T^{\prime})|=2|H_{L,L^{\prime}}(\delta)-H_{L,L^{\prime}}(0)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2|\int_{-\infty}^{\delta}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(\delta-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2|\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(\delta-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})-e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t|

\displaystyle+2|\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(\delta-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t|=\hat{H}_{1}+\hat{H}_{2}

For the first term, note that
s<t-t^{\prime}
, by Proposition
B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{1}\leq 2(e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}|\delta}-1)H_{L,L^{\prime}}(0)\leq F\frac{1\wedge|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}|\delta|}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}+a)}

For the second, note that
t^{\prime}\leq t
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(\delta-t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\leq F\cdot\frac{e^{-|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}(\delta-t)-at}}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}+a}

by Proposition
B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{2}\leq F\cdot(\frac{\delta}{|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}+a}\wedge\frac{1}{(|\psi(L)|^{\gamma}+a)^{2}})

Summary above two bounds, then the claim is proved.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Fix
H
can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\exp(-a|s-s^{\prime}|-b|s-t|-c|t^{\prime}-s^{\prime}|-d|t^{\prime}-s|-e|t-s^{\prime}|)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s

where
a,b,c,d,e>0
,
a+e\neq b
and
H(0)\leq F
. Fix
\hat{H}(t,t^{\prime})=H(t,t)+H(t^{\prime},t^{\prime})-H(t,t^{\prime})-H(t^{\prime},t)
, then
\hat{H}
has estimate

\displaystyle|\hat{H}(t,t^{\prime})|\lesssim F(e^{(-(b+d)|t-t^{\prime}|)}-1)+\frac{e^{-(b+d)(t-t^{\prime})}}{a+c+d}(\frac{1-e^{-(a+e-b)|t-t^{\prime}|}}{a+e-b}).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
t>t^{\prime}
. Rewrite
H(t,t^{\prime})
as follows

\displaystyle H(t,t^{\prime})=\int_{-\infty}^{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}

\displaystyle\exp(-a|s-s^{\prime}|-b(t-t^{\prime}-s)-c(-s^{\prime})-d|-s|-e(t-t^{\prime}-s^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s

Fix
K(t-t^{\prime},s,s^{\prime})
is the inner part of the integral, then we calculate that

\displaystyle|\hat{H}(t,t^{\prime})|=

\displaystyle|2H(t,t^{\prime})-H(t,t)-H(s,s)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2|\int_{-\infty}^{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}K(t-t^{\prime};s^{\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}K(0;s^{\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2|\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}K(t-t^{\prime};s^{\prime},s)-K(0;s^{\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s|

\displaystyle+2|\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}K(t-t^{\prime};s^{\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s|

For the second term, note that
s>s^{\prime}
holds throughout the domain of integration. We calculate that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}K(t-t^{\prime};s^{\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s=\frac{e^{-(b+e)(t-t^{\prime})}}{a+c+e}e^{-(a+d)s+b(t-t^{\prime}-s)}\lesssim\frac{1\wedge e^{-(a+d)s+b(t-t^{\prime}-s)}}{a+c+e}

Then by Proposition
B.1
, we have the estimate that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}K(t-t^{\prime};s^{\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\frac{1\wedge(a+d+b)|t-t^{\prime}|}{(a+c+e)(a+d+b)}

On another way,
where for the first term, we use the fact
K(t-t^{\prime};s^{\prime},s):=\exp{(-(b+e)(t-t^{\prime}))}K(0;s^{\prime},s)
. Then by Proposition
B.1
, we have

\displaystyle|\hat{H}(t,t^{\prime})|\lesssim F(e^{(-(b+e)(t-t^{\prime}))}-1)+\frac{e^{-(b+e)(t-t^{\prime})}}{a+c+e}(\frac{1-e^{-(a+d-b)(t-t^{\prime})}}{a+d+b}).

Since
t
and
t^{\prime}
is symmetric, the claim is proved.
∎

Proposition B.2
.

Fix
R_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime}):=\mathbb{E}\bigg(Y_{k}\otimes Y_{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{I}_{L_{\downarrow},L^{\prime}_{\downarrow}}((Y_{l}Y_{m})\otimes(Y_{l^{\prime}}Y_{m^{\prime}}))(t,t^{\prime})\bigg)
, then it can be written as

\displaystyle R_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}R_{P^{i};L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime}).

where
R_{LL^{\prime}}{(P^{1};T,T^{\prime})}
and
R_{LL^{\prime}}{(P^{2};T,T^{\prime})}
is satisfied the estimate

\displaystyle R_{LL^{\prime}}(P^{1};t,t^{\prime})\leq\frac{|(l+m)|^{2}|k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}e^{-|k|^{\gamma}|t-t^{\prime}|}}{|l+m|^{\gamma}(|l+m|^{\gamma}+(|l|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}))}

and

\displaystyle R_{LL^{\prime}}(P^{2};t,t^{\prime})\leq\frac{|(l+m)||l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}||k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}e^{-|k|^{\gamma}\wedge|l|^{\gamma}|t-t^{\prime}|}}{(|l+m|^{\gamma}+|l|^{\gamma})(|k+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})}

And
R_{LL^{\prime}}{(P^{3};T,T^{\prime})}
satisfied

\displaystyle\sum_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};t,t^{\prime})\lesssim\frac{1}{|m|^{4\gamma-6}}

for some small enough
\kappa
.

Proof.

To simplify our notation, we fix
L=(k,l,m)
is a triple in
\mathbb{Z}_{*}^{3}
,
C_{L}^{\epsilon}=C_{\epsilon}(k)C_{\epsilon}(l)C_{\epsilon}(m)
and
e_{L}(x)=e^{i\psi(L)x}
. Rewrite
R_{L,L^{\prime}}(T,T^{\prime})
as

\displaystyle C_{L}^{\epsilon}C_{L^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-|l+m|^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-|l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})}|l+m||l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}|\mathbb{E}[Y_{k}(t)Y_{l}(s)Y_{m}(s)Y_{k^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})Y_{l^{\prime}}(s^{\prime})Y_{m^{\prime}}(s^{\prime})],

By Wick theorem
4.4
, it can be calculated by such pairing

(1)(k,k^{\prime}),\>(l,l^{\prime}),\>(m,m^{\prime}),\quad(2)(k,l^{\prime}),\>(k^{\prime},l),\>(m,m^{\prime}),\quad(3)(k,l),\>(k^{\prime},l^{\prime}),\>(m,m^{\prime})

(B.1)

Remark B.1
.

Let us give some remark as this pairing. We omit some case of pairing, like
(k,m)
since
m
and
l
is symmetric, and there isn’t occurred pair like
(l,m)
since we assume that
l+m\neq 0
.

By the covariance for the unit
Y
, fix

\displaystyle|k|^{\gamma-1}|l|^{\gamma-1}|m|^{\gamma-1}\mathcal{I}_{1}=

\displaystyle e^{-[|k|^{\gamma}|t-t^{\prime}|+(|l|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma})|s-s^{\prime}|]}\textbf{1}_{k+k^{\prime}=0}\textbf{1}_{l+l^{\prime}=0}\textbf{1}_{m+m^{\prime}=0}

\displaystyle|k|^{\gamma-1}|l|^{\gamma-1}|m|^{\gamma-1}\mathcal{I}_{2}=

\displaystyle e^{-[|k|^{\gamma}(t-s^{\prime})+|l|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s)+|m|^{\gamma}|s-s^{\prime}|]}\textbf{1}_{k+l^{\prime}=0}\textbf{1}_{k^{\prime}+l=0}\textbf{1}_{m+m^{\prime}=0}

\displaystyle|k|^{\gamma-1}|k^{\prime}|^{\gamma-1}|m|^{\gamma-1}\mathcal{I}_{3}=

\displaystyle e^{-[|k|^{\gamma}(t-s)+|k^{\prime}|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})+|m|^{\gamma}|s-s^{\prime}|]}\textbf{1}_{k+l=0}\textbf{1}_{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}=0}\textbf{1}_{m+m^{\prime}=0}

We label the aforementioned pairing schemes as
P_{1}
,
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
, and for simply our calculation, we fix the integral as
\mathcal{R}_{LL^{\prime}}(P^{i};t,t^{\prime})
without the weight caused by resonant operator
\circ
, and use
\mathcal{R}_{LL^{\prime}}(P^{i};t,t^{\prime})
,
i=1,2,3
represent the original one which means

\mathcal{R}_{LL^{\prime}}(t,t^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{R}_{LL^{\prime}}(P^{i};t,t^{\prime}).

For
\mathcal{R}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{1};t,t^{\prime})
, by Lemma
B.1
, we have

\displaystyle R_{LL^{\prime}}(P^{1};t,t^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-|k|^{\gamma}|t-t^{\prime}|}}{|l+m|^{\gamma}(|l+m|^{\gamma}+(|l|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}))}|(l+m)|^{2}|k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}

By Proposition
B.1
, we can estimate that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{2};t,t^{\prime})=

\displaystyle|(l+m)||l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}||k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-|l+m|^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-|k+m|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})}e^{-\mathcal{I}_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-|l+m|^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-|k+m|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})-|k|^{\gamma}(t-s^{\prime})-|l|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s)-|m|^{\gamma}|s-s^{\prime}|}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{|(l+m)||l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}||k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}e^{-|k|^{\gamma}\wedge|l|^{\gamma}|t-t^{\prime}|}}{(|l+m|^{\gamma}+|l|^{\gamma})(|k+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})+|m|^{\gamma}(|l+m|^{\gamma}+|l|^{\gamma})\wedge(|k+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{|(l+m)||l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}||k|^{1-\gamma}|l|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{1-\gamma}e^{-|k|^{\gamma}\wedge|l|^{\gamma}|t-t^{\prime}|}}{(|l+m|^{\gamma}+|l|^{\gamma})(|k+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})}.

It’s not easy to estimate the third integral
\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}^{(3)}(s,s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s
by a directly calculation, so we use the symmetric for
L
and
L^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. In fact, for any
L
and
L^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, we can divide
\sum_{L,L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}
to two parts

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};t,t^{\prime})=

\displaystyle|kk^{\prime}m|^{1-\gamma}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-|l+m|^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-|l^{\prime}+m^{\prime}|^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})}e^{-\mathcal{I}_{3}}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|kk^{\prime}m|^{1-\gamma}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-(|k-m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})(t-s)}e^{-(|k^{\prime}+m|^{\gamma}+|k^{\prime}|^{\gamma})(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})-|m|^{\gamma}|s-s^{\prime}|}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

Use following Lemma

Lemma B.3
.

Fix
\mathcal{H}_{k,m}(t,s)=(k+m)e^{-(|k-m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})(t-s)}+(k-m)e^{-(|k+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})(t-s)}
, then there exists a constant
c>0
, such that the bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{k,m}(t,s)\lesssim e^{-c(|k+m|+|m|)^{\gamma}(t-s)}|m|^{(2-\frac{\gamma}{2})\epsilon}(|k|+|k+m|)^{(2-\frac{\gamma}{2})(1-\epsilon)}.

is hold for any
k\neq m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

By a directly computation we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\mathcal{R}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};t,t^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\sum_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}|kk^{\prime}m|^{1-\gamma}\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{k,m}(t,s)\mathcal{H}_{k^{\prime},m}(t^{\prime},s^{\prime})e^{-|m|^{\gamma}|s-s^{\prime}|}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}|kk^{\prime}m|^{1-\gamma}|m|^{(4-\gamma)\epsilon}(|k^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}+m|)^{(2-\frac{\gamma}{2})(1-\epsilon)}(|k|+|k+m|)^{(2-\frac{\gamma}{2})(1-\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-c(|k+m|+|k|)^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-c(|k^{\prime}+m|+|k|)^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})}e^{-|m|^{\gamma}|s-s^{\prime}|}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

By Hölder inequality, we estimate the integral that

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-c(|k+m|+|k|)^{\gamma}(t-s)}e^{-c(|k^{\prime}+m|+|k|)^{\gamma}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})}e^{-|m|^{\gamma}|s-s^{\prime}|}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(|k+m|+|k|)^{-\gamma\theta}|m|^{-\gamma(1-\theta)}(|k^{\prime}+m|+|k^{\prime}|)^{-\gamma}\wedge(|k^{\prime}+m|+|k^{\prime}|)^{-\gamma\bar{\theta}}|m|^{-\gamma(1-\bar{\theta})}(|k+m|+|k|)^{-\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(|k+m|+|k|)^{\alpha_{1}}(|k^{\prime}+m|+|k^{\prime}|)^{\alpha_{2}}|m|^{\alpha_{3}}}

where
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=2\gamma
. Add the weight, by Lemma
4.1
, we have the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\mathcal{R}_{L,L^{\prime}}(P^{3};t,t^{\prime})\lesssim\sum_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{|kk^{\prime}m|^{1-\gamma}}{(|k+m|+|k|)^{\beta_{1}}(|k^{\prime}+m|+|k^{\prime}|)^{\beta_{2}}|m|^{\beta_{3}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|m|^{4\gamma-6}},

which the claim is proved by fixing
\beta_{1}=\frac{1}{2}
,
\beta_{2}=\frac{1}{2}
,
\beta_{3}=3\gamma-5
.
∎

Finally, we give prove Lemma
B.3

proof of Lemma
B.3
.

By a directly computation, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{k,m}(t,s)=

\displaystyle k(e^{-(|k-m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})(t-s)}+e^{-(|k-m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})(t-s)})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m(e^{-(|k-m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})(t-s)}-e^{-(|k+m|^{\gamma}+|k|^{\gamma})(t-s)})

where we use the identity

\displaystyle ac+bd=\frac{1}{2}((a+b)(c+d)-(a-b)(c-d))

Note that
|e^{-x}-e^{-y}|\leq(1\wedge|x-y|)e^{-x\wedge y}
. We obtain the bound

\displaystyle|\mathcal{H}_{k,m}(t,s)|\leq e^{-||k|-|m||^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}|t-s|}(2|k|+|m|(1\wedge 4|km||t-s|))

Note that
||k|-|m||^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}>c|k+m|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}
, for some
c\geq\frac{1}{3}
. This implies that

\displaystyle|t-s|e^{-||k|-|m||^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}|t-s|}\lesssim\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{3}(|k|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma})}}{|k|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}}

then we have the bound

\displaystyle|\mathcal{H}_{k,m}(t,s)|\lesssim e^{-|k+m|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}|t-s|}(2|k|+|m|(1\wedge 4\frac{|km|}{|k|^{\gamma}+|m|^{\gamma}})).

∎
